For gradient, sum over first nondiag (1 - h_j) + diag (1 - 2h_i?) plus second -h_j? Wait c_m = sum over pairs. The derivative wrt h_i of c_m for that pair if i first nondiag: ∂(h_i - h_i h_j)=1 - h_j. If i second: ∂(-h_j h_i) = -h_j. If diag: ∂(h_i - h_i^2)=1 - 2h_i. The A used for coordinate recentering has diag 1, but gradient has diag 1-2h. For smooth objective gradient, we need derivative of current c values: diag term 1-2h_i.
But we can use A structure: For coordinate i, A_i (excluding diag quadratic) has for diagonal +1, nondiag first (1-h_j), second -h_j. If we added c0? Gradient = A_i - 2h_i*diag? For diag A_i=1, derivative diag =1 -2h_i. So gradient_i = A_i - diag_indicator 2h_i. Equivalent: using first/second lists with diag excluded, plus diag coeff 1-2h_i.
For smooth max gradient w_m: grad_i = ∑_{m} w_m ∂c_m/∂h_i = ∑_{first nondiag pairs with lag m} w_m (1 - h_j) + ∑_{second nondiag} w_m (-h_j) + w_diag (1 - 2h_i).
Thus we can compute grad by accumulating weighted values over lag weights w. We can precompute per i first_lags, first_partner, second_lags, second_partner. For each i, grad[i] = np.sum(w[first_lags] * (1 - h[first_partner])) + np.sum(-w[second_lags]*h[second_partner]) + w[d]*(1-2h_i).
This is O(n) with vector sums per i. Could vectorize across all i using sparse? Or loop i but n=1000, each vector length ~n, O(n^2) per gradient ~1M; for many iterations maybe heavy but okay if ~100 gradients=100M operations. Python loops over i with numpy sums could be slow (1000*100=100k vector sum calls length ~1000, maybe 1s? Actually 100k*~2us=0.2s, okay; with Python overhead maybe 1s). We can optimize by using correlation of weighted lags? Let's derive faster.

The first contribution: ∑_i ∑_{m in first_i} w_m (1 - h_j(i,m)). For each lag m, it contributes to many i. Could compute using convolutions: first: w_m *1 for h_i coefficient plus -w_m * h_j. The derivative wrt h_i from all pairs can be expressed as:
grad_i = w[d]*(1-2h_i) + ∑_{j≠i} (w_{|?} maybe orientation) * [indicator i first *1 - h_j]. Let's map pairs: For each pair (i,j) with i≠j, there is an output lag m. If i is first in that lag, grad_i += w_m; grad_j? If j is second in same lag, grad_j += -w_m h_i? That's handled by second list of j. Thus we can compute two components:
F_i = sum of weights of lags where i is first (nondiag). This is independent of h: F_i = ∑_{m in first_lags[i]} w_m.
S_i = sum over lags where i is second of w_m * h_partner(i,m) = ∑_{j} w_{lag(i,j)} h_j.
Then grad_i = F_i - S_i + w_d(1-2h_i).
F_i can be computed by adding w_m to each i that is first at lag m. For each m, first positions i? From first range: m, partner j=i+n-1-m. i = m - (n-1) + j. For fixed m, i valid range? This is like shifting: first contribution +w_m applies to i where partner in [0,n). The set of i first at lag m is an interval. We can compute F by accumulating w_m over intervals: difference array O(n). Similarly S_i = ∑ w_m h_{i+n-1-m}? For fixed m, S_i = w_m * h_{j} where j = i+n-1-m. So S is a shifted copy of h scaled by w_m added to interval. Could implement via loops over m (M~2n) and vector slices: for each m, first positions i range [max(0,m-(n-1))? Wait j=i+n-1-m => i = m - (n-1) + j. For j∈[0,n), i range [m-(n-1), m] intersect [0,n-1]. That's length ~min. For each m, add w_m to F[i_start:i_end+1] and S[i_start:i_end+1] += w_m * h[j corresponding]. The j array is reversed relative to i: j = i + (n-1) - m. Could vector add slice. Similarly second: for fixed m, i second? j = m-(n-1)+i, i range? S2_i = w_m * h_j. So also slice with h shifted. Total O(M * length) if vector? We can use np.add? Maybe simpler to stick with per-i arrays; n=500 fine.

Alternatively, gradient can be computed via correlations of w? Let's derive using shifts of w.
Given output lag m (0..M-1). Leading index? For first: pair (i,j) with j = i + n-1 - m. That means lag m corresponds to offset d = j - i = n-1 - m. So if m < n-1, d positive (j>i); if m > n-1, d negative. For first nondiag at lag m, i ranges such that both valid. The contribution to grad_i is 1 for each i that is first; S_i is -h_j. In terms of offset d: for d>0 (j=i+d), lag m=n-1-d. For first, i=0..n-d-1; grad_i += w_{n-1-d}; S_i += h_{i+d}. For d<0 (j=i+d, i from -d..n-1); lag m=n-1-d >n-1; first i range -d..n-1; S_i += h_{i+d}. So F_i = sum over d≠0 such that i and i+d valid of |w_{n-1-d}|? Actually weight same. This is a Toeplitz sum. Can compute F by convolution: F_i = ∑_{d} w_{n-1-d} 1_{valid}. For d>0 intervals, add w to prefix. Similarly S = ∑ w_{n-1-d} h_{i+d}. This is cross-correlation of w with h? Specifically S_i = ∑_{d} w_{n-1-d} h_{i+d} for d where valid. Let k = -d? This is a convolution of w (indexed by m) and h. Could compute via FFT or direct np.correlate? n small. But per gradient maybe simpler to precompute weights w and compute F/S using loops over d (n), each adding slices O(n) => O(n^2), but vectorized slices efficient.

Maybe custom smooth gradient not needed; exact CD uses A per coordinate with similar O(n^2). Smooth AD can accelerate global.

Implementation of make_struct efficient:
def make_struct(n):
    M=2*n-1; d=n-1
    fidx=[]; fpar=[]; sidx=[]; spl=[]
    mint64 = np.int64
    for p in range(n):
        lf = max(0, p); rf = min(M-1, p+n-1)
        if lf <= rf:
            ks = np.arange(lf, rf+1, dtype=int)
            js = p + d - ks
            valid = (js>=0)&(js<n)
            # exclude diag ks==d (js==p)
            nd = valid & (ks != d)
            fidx.append(ks[nd]); fpar.append(js[nd])
        else: empty
        ls = max(0, d-p); rs = min(M-1, M-1-p)
        if ls<=rs:
            ks = np.arange(ls, rs+1, dtype=int)
            js = ks - d + p
            valid=(js>=0)&(js<n)
            nd=valid & (ks != d)
            sidx.append(ks[nd]); spl.append(js[nd])
    return fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M
This loops n and arange per p O(n^2) okay for n up to 1200 (1.4M). Memory: lists of arrays. Could optimize by using int32? np.int64 default. 1M*8=8MB; 4 arrays 32MB; okay. For n=2000 64MB maybe too high. Limit n to maybe 1000/1200. 

Could store as int32 to reduce: dtype=np.int32. Use np.arange(..., dtype=np.int32). js may be int32. Good.

def make_A(h, p, n, d, fidx, fpar, sidx, spl):
    M=2*n-1
    A = np.zeros(M, dtype=float)
    fi = fidx[p]; if fi.size: A[fi] += 1.0 - h[fpar[p]]
    si = sidx[p]; if si.size: A[si] += -h[spl[p]]
    A[d] = 1.0? Need add? The first/second excluded diag, so A[d]=1.0. But if there are non-diag contributions at d? No. Set A[d]=1.0. Could there be first/second contributions to d with partner not p? For full correlate diag output only pairs i=i; for fixed p, only p. So set.
    return A

Implement evaluate_t(c,A,d,h0,t,temp):
    delta = t - h0
    np.multiply(A, delta, out=temp)
    temp += c
    temp[d] -= t*t - h0*h0
    return float(np.max(temp))

def cd_fast(h, c, n, struct, deadline, passes=10000, grid_full=24, golden=24, quick_points=(0.0,0.5,1.0), tol=1e-11):
    fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
    temp = np.empty(M)
    # work arrays for A? A allocated each coordinate.
    order = np.arange(n)
    improved_any=False
    for pass in range(passes):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        perm = order? We can use random perm each pass? Generate via rng? Top-level rng passed. But cd_fast shouldn't require rng? Could accept rng. Simpler use cyclic order; maybe random to avoid stuck. We can create perm = np.arange(n); for each pass maybe perm = (perm + step)?? Use rng permutation if rng passed. rng.permutation(n) O(n) cheap. But no closures.
        current_max = float(np.max(c))
        improved=0
        for p in perm:
            if time.time() > deadline - 1: break
            h0 = float(h[p])
            A = np.zeros(M)
            fi=fidx[p]; fpi=fpar[p]
            if fi.size: A[fi] += 1.0 - h[fpi]
            si=sidx[p]; spi=spl[p]
            if si.size: A[si] += -h[spi]
            A[d]=1.0
            # quick ts: 0, h0? f(h0)=current_max. If h0 not 0/1, include h0? not improve. Include 0.25,0.5,0.75,1.
            best_q = current_max; best_tq = h0
            for tq in quick_ts: # tuple top-level? Could define [0,0.5,1] inside from parameter? Avoid allocation? Use constant list global? Top-level quick_ts = (0.0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0) okay.
                val=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,tq,temp)
                if val < best_q: best_q=val; best_tq=tq
            if best_q >= current_max - cd_tol: continue
            # full grid
            grid = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid_full)  # allocated per coordinate? bad. Precompute global grid array.
            best_g=current_max; best_tg=h0
            # evaluate grid maybe with loop; if tq maybe interior.
            for tg in grid:
               val=eval_t(...); if val<best_g ...
            # golden around best_g index: if best_tg at endpoint skip? run if interior.
            # We need know index. Store.
            if 0 < gi < grid_full-1:
                lo=grid[gi-1]; hi=grid[gi+1]
                # golden minimize f on [lo,hi] (not necessarily unimodal but okay). Use t0/t1.
                # evaluate using eval_t.
                # after golden, update best.
            # choose best_t/best_f; if best_f < current_max - tol:
                update c,h; current_max=best_f; improved++
        if improved:
            h=project_feasible(h,n)
            c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float)
        else:
            break
    return h,c

Implement:
def project_feasible(h,n):
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   s=h.sum()
   target=0.5*n
   diff=target-s
   if abs(diff)<1e-15 return h
   if diff>0:
       room=n-s
       if room<=0 return h
       alpha=diff/room
       h = h + alpha*(1-h)
   else:
       if s<=0 return h
       alpha=diff/s
       h = h*(1+alpha)   # since 1+alpha = 1+diff/s = target/s
   return np.clip(h,0,1)

import numpy as np
import time

Pseudo:
def line_search(c,A,d,h0,current_max,grid,temp,golden_iters):
    best_f=current_max; best_t=h0
    m=grid.size; best_i=0
    # maybe avoid evaluating t=0 and1 if known? But grid includes.
    for i,t in enumerate(grid):
        f=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,t,temp)
        if f < best_f:
           best_f=f; best_t=float(t); best_i=i
    if 0 < best_i < m-1:
       lo=grid[best_i-1]; hi=grid[best_i+1]
       # golden minimize eval_t on [lo,hi]
       invphi=(np.sqrt(5)-1)/2
       a=lo; b=hi
       c1=b-invphi*(b-a); d1=a+invphi*(b-a)
       fc1=eval_t(...c1); fd1=eval_t(...d1)
       for _ in range(golden_iters):
           if fc1 < fd1:
               b=d1; d1=c1; fd1=fc1; c1=b-invphi*(b-a); fc1=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,c1,temp)
           else:
               a=c1; c1=d1; fc1=fd1; d1=a+invphi*(b-a); fd1=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,d1,temp)
       tm=(a+b)*0.5
       fm=eval_t(...tm)
       if fm<best_f: best_f=fm; best_t=tm
       # also check c1/d1? Their values fc1/fd1 maybe; update if min
       if fc1<best_f: ... but fc1 from last? Could keep candidates list? Simpler evaluate endpoints a,b? not.
    # Evaluate endpoints 0 and1 maybe if not grid? grid includes.
    return best_t,best_f
But golden assumes unimodal and doesn't evaluate global at candidate if f midpoint not best. The grid best value is retained. Good.
If function nonunimodal, golden may miss but grid min retained. Use golden only to improve within bracket; if bracket has two minima and high middle, it may converge to one boundary? But grid best likely endpoint of bracket; okay.

cd_fast:
```
def cd_fast(h, c, n, struct, deadline, grid_size=24, golden_iters=20, project_every=1, rng=None):
    fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
    temp=np.empty(M)
    grid=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid_size)
    tol=1e-11
    target=0.5*n
    passes=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        perm = np.arange(n)
        if rng is not None:
            perm = rng.permutation(n)
        current_max = float(np.max(c))
        improved=0
        for p in perm:
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            h0=float(h[p])
            A=np.zeros(M)
            fi=fidx[p]
            if fi.size: A[fi] += 1.0 - h[fpar[p]]
            si=sidx[p]
            if si.size: A[si] += -h[spl[p]]
            A[d]=1.0
            # quick check
            best_quick=current_max; tq=h0
            # if h0 not 0 or1 maybe t=h0 current, but we can still test endpoints and mid
            for t in QUICK_TS:
                f=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,t,temp)
                if f < best_quick:
                    best_quick=f; tq=t
            if best_quick >= current_max - tol:
                continue
            best_t,best_f = line_search(c,A,d,h0,current_max,grid,temp,golden_iters)
            if best_f < current_max - tol and best_t != h0:
                delta=best_t-h0
                if abs(delta) > 1e-14:
                    c += delta*A
                    c[d] -= best_t*best_t - h0*h0
                    h[p]=best_t
                    current_max = float(np.max(c)) # or best_f but after update maybe best_f
                    # due to float, if np.max > best_f? use max(best_f,?)
                    if current_max > best_f:
                        current_max = best_f? Actually c update should exact, but round; use current_max=float(np.max(c)) safe. This is O(M) per accepted; okay.
                    improved +=1
        if improved:
            h = project_feasible(h,n)
            c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h, mode='full').astype(float)
            passes +=1
        else:
            # no coordinate improves; try a small random perturbation? Not inside; break.
            break
    return h,c
```

Interpolate:
```
def interpolate_h(h_old,n_old,n_new):
    if n_new==len(h_old): return h_old.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_old)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_new)
    h_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,h_old)
    return project_feasible(h_new,n_new)
```
This duplicates x arrays; for n=1000 okay. Could use np.linspace each time.

Function compute_grad top-level:
```
def compute_grad(h,c,n,struct,weights):
   fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
   dx=2.0/n
   grad=np.empty(n)
   wd=weights[d]
   for p in range(n):
      g=wd*(1.0-2.0*h[p])
      fi=fidx[p]
      if fi.size:
          g += np.dot(weights[fi], (1.0 - h[fpar[p]]))  # dot maybe faster
      si=sidx[p]
      if si.size:
          g -= np.dot(weights[si], h[spl[p]])
      grad[p]=dx*g
   return grad
```
Using np.dot with arrays. weights[fi] and arrays allocate. Could use np.sum. Fine.
But weights length M. For each p, weights[fi] advanced indexing. Could precompute? Not.

Implementation details for AD:
We need smooth_ad top-level. But time budget and complexity. Maybe we can omit to reduce code size and risk. But "do something different" encourages AD. We can include a modest AD. Need ensure no closures. Let's write:

```
def compute_grad_weights(h, c, n, struct, weights):
    fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
    dx=2.0/n
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    wd=weights[d]
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    for p in range(n):
        g = wd * (1.0 - 2.0*h[p])
        fi=fidx[p]
        if fi.size:
            partner = fpar[p]
            g += np.dot(weights[fi], 1.0 - h[partner])
        si=sidx[p]
        if si.size:
            partner = spl[p]
            g -= np.dot(weights[si], h[partner])
        grad[p] = dx * g
    return grad
```
Potential speed: `1.0 - h[partner]` alloc; could `np.dot(weights[fi], h[fpar[p]])` and add sum weights: g += wfi_sum - dot(weights[fi], h[partner]). Precompute sum_weights_per_lag? weights changes. Could do:
```
wfi = weights[fi]
g += wfi.sum() - np.dot(wfi, h[partner])
```
But wfi.sum alloc? no. Similarly second: g -= np.dot(weights[si], h[partner]). This uses dot. Fine.

`smooth_ad`:
```
def smooth_ad(h, c, n, struct, deadline):
    h=h.copy(); c=c.copy()
    target=0.5*n
    mom=np.zeros(n); var=np.zeros(n)
    lr=0.03
    betas=(2.0,5.0,10.0,30.0,100.0,300.0)
    best=compute_c5(h,n)
    for beta in betas:
       max_iter=40
       for it in range(max_iter):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float)
          val=c*(2.0/n)
          mv=float(np.max(val))
          if mv<=0: return h,c
          e=np.exp(beta*(val-mv))
          w=e/e.sum()
          grad=compute_grad_weights(h,c,n,struct,w)
          # backtracking Adam step
          accepted=False
          for _ in range(8):
              h_try = h - lr*(mom)/(np.sqrt(var)+1e-8)? Need update mom/var before step? Standard:
              m = β1 m + (1-β1)g; v=β2 v+(1-β2)g*g; uns=...
              h_try = h - lr*uns
              h_try = project_feasible(h_try,n)
              c_try = np.correlate(h_try,1-h_try)
              ctv=float(np.max(c_try))
              if ctv < float(np.max(c)) or it==0? 
                  accept break
              lr *=0.6
          # But v depends on grad, compute once.
          if not accept: break beta? 
          h=...
    return h,c
```

Let's write line_search carefully:
```
def line_search(c, A, d, h0, current_max, grid, temp, golden_iters):
    m = grid.size
    best_f = current_max
    best_t = float(h0)
    best_i = 0
    for i in range(m):
        t = float(grid[i])
        f = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, t, temp)
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f; best_t = t; best_i = i
    if 0 < best_i < m - 1 and best_f < current_max:
        lo = float(grid[best_i - 1]); hi = float(grid[best_i + 1])
        invphi = (np.sqrt(5.0) - 1.0) / 2.0
        a = lo; b = hi
        c1 = b - invphi * (b - a)
        d1 = a + invphi * (b - a)
        fc1 = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, c1, temp)
        fd1 = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, d1, temp)
        for _ in range(golden_iters):
            if fc1 < fd1:
                b = d1; d1 = c1; fd1 = fc1
                c1 = b - invphi * (b - a)
                fc1 = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, c1, temp)
            else:
                a = c1; c1 = d1; fc1 = fd1
                d1 = a + invphi * (b - a)
                fd1 = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, d1, temp)
        tm = 0.5 * (a + b)
        fm = eval_t(c, A, d, h0, tm, temp)
        if fm < best_f:
            best_f = fm; best_t = float(tm)
        # also consider last c1/d1 maybe
        if fc1 < best_f:
            best_f = fc1; best_t = float(c1)
        if fd1 < best_f:
            best_f = fd1; best_t = float(d1)
    return best_t, best_f
```
If best_i=0 or m-1, no golden. But golden may improve near endpoint? If best at endpoint, function decreasing/increasing to endpoint; no interior better if grid best. Could be just endpoint local but interior slightly better not captured if grid not fine? If best at endpoint 0 but f(0.05) lower and f(0.2) higher, grid best might still 0 if f0 lower; no. AD code concise:
```
def smooth_restart(h, n, struct, deadline):
    h = h.copy(); target=0.5*n
    lr=0.5
    betas=(3.0,10.0,30.0,100.0)
    for beta in betas:
       for _ in range(20):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float)
          cur = float(np.max(c))
          vals=c*(2.0/n)
          m=vals.max()
          w=np.exp(beta*(vals-m)); w/=w.sum()
          # compute grad
          fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
          dx=2.0/n; grad=np.zeros(n); wd=w[d]
          for p in range(n):
             g=wd*(1.0-2.0*h[p])
             fi=fidx[p]
             if fi.size:
                wf=w[fi]
                g += float(np.sum(wf)) - float(np.dot(wf, h[fpar[p]]))
             si=sidx[p]
             if si.size:
                g -= float(np.dot(w[si], h[spl[p]]))
             grad[p]=dx*g
          # gradient descent with backtracking
          step=lr
          accepted=False
          for _ in range(10):
             h2=project_feasible(h - step*grad, n)
             c2=np.correlate(h2,1.0-h2).astype(float)
             mx=float(np.max(c2))
             if mx < cur:
                h=h2; c=c2; accepted=True; break
             step *=0.5
          if not accepted: break
    return h, c
```
This is ~45 lines. It repeatedly recomputes c and gradient O(n^2). For n=500, 20*4*500 dot loops; maybe 120 iterations; okay (maybe 10s). For n=1000 maybe 40s. Use at n0 only with deadline 60s. It may find improvements or basin. Add run call after initial CD:
```

    h_ad, c_ad = smooth_restart(best_h, len(best_h), struct_best, time.time()+min(60, remaining/3))
    c_ad_max = float(np.max(c_ad))*2/n
    if c_ad_max < best_c: best_h,...
```

Function `active_search(h, c, n, deadline, max_iter=200, k=48, lr_start=0.05)`:
```
def active_search(h,c,n,deadline,k=48):
    h=h.copy(); c=np.correlate(...); target? project already.
    cur=float(np.max(c))
    lr=0.05
    dx=2.0/n
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time>deadline: break
       # select active lags
       # Use c values; top k = min(k, len(c))
       kk = min(k, c.size)
       # maybe include only lags with c > cur - 1.0 to avoid irrelevant. Use argpartition.
       if kk < c.size:
           idx = np.argpartition(c, -kk)[-kk:]
       else: idx = np.arange(c.size)
       # weights uniform or exp
       # Compute softmax: a = c[idx] * 20? Use beta=20? c range maybe 100; differences 1. beta=5 enough.
       am = c[idx] - cur  # <=0
       w = np.exp(5.0*am)
       sw = w.sum()
       if sw <=0: w=np.ones(kk); sw=kk
       else: w /= sw
       grad=np.zeros(n)
       d=n-1
       for m,wi in zip(idx,w):
           if wi <= 1e-12: continue
           diff = d - m   # d? For lag m, first offset = n-1 - m = d - m.
           if diff >= 0:
               L = n - diff
               grad[0:L] += wi * (1.0 - h[diff:n])
               grad[diff:n] -= wi * h[0:L]
           else:
               L = n + diff
               grad[-diff:n] += wi * (1.0 - h[0:L])
               grad[0:L] -= wi * h[-diff:n]
           # Note for diff=0, the second uses grad[0:n] -= wi*h[0:n]; fine.
       grad *= dx
       # backtracking
       best_step=0; accepted=False
       try_lr=lr
       for _ in range(10):
           h2 = project_feasible(h - try_lr*grad, n)
           c2 = np.correlate(h2,1.0-h2).astype(float)
           mx=float(np.max(c2))
           if mx < cur - 1e-13:
              h=h2; c=c2; cur=mx; lr=min(0.5, try_lr*1.2); accepted=True; break
           try_lr *=0.5
       if not accepted:
           # try opposite direction? We can also try h + small*grad if descent fails
           try_lr=lr*0.1
           for _ in range(5):
              h2=project_feasible(h + try_lr*grad, n)
              c2=...
              if mx < cur: accept; break
              try_lr*=0.5
           if not accepted: break
    return h,c,cur
```

But active gradient only top lags may not reduce all; backtracking ensures objective decreases at each accepted step (computed exact C count). So it's a valid descent (subgradient projection not exact but line search ensures improvement). It may get stuck if no step lowers with try_lr. Use break.

Now code details for active_search:
```
def active_search(h, c, n, deadline, max_iter=500, k=32, lr_init=2.0):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    if c is None: c = ...
    cur = float(np.max(c))
    lr = lr_init
    d = n-1
    consecutive_no=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        # Ensure c current? we maintain after accept.
        # top k
        kk = k if k < c.size else c.size
        if kk < c.size:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -kk)[-kk:]
        else:
            idx = np.arange(c.size)
        # weights
        am = c[idx] - cur
        w = np.exp(6.0 * am)
        sw = w.sum()
        if sw <= 1e-300:
            w = np.full_like(idx, 1.0, dtype=float); sw=float(idx.size)
        else:
            w = w / sw
        grad = np.zeros(n)
        for m_i in range(len(idx)):
            wi = float(w[m_i])
            if wi < 1e-12: continue
            m = int(idx[m_i]); diff = d - m
            if diff >= 0:
                L = n - diff
                if L > 0:
                    grad[0:L] += wi * (1.0 - h[diff:n])
                    grad[diff:n] -= wi * h[0:L]
            else:
                L = n + diff
                if L > 0:
                    grad[-diff:n] += wi * (1.0 - h[0:L])
                    grad[0:L] -= wi * h[-diff:n]
        grad *= 2.0 / n
        # Try descent with backtracking
        step = lr
        accepted = False
        for _ in range(6):
            h2 = project_feasible(h - step * grad, n)
            c2 = np.correlate(h2, 1.0 - h2, mode='full').astype(float)
            mx = float(np.max(c2))
            if mx < cur - 1e-12:
                h = h2; c = c2; cur = mx; lr = min(10.0, step * 1.3); consecutive_no=0; accepted=True; break
            step *= 0.5
        if not accepted:
            # try ascent/different? Since gradient descent maybe local but line search fail, break after some count
            consecutive_no += 1
            if consecutive_no > 5:
                break
            # try a smaller randomizada? no
    return h, c, cur
```
If gradient zero (all max? maybe uniform?), step fail and break. Good.
`np.argpartition(c, -kk)` for kk close size; if kk=size, use arange. If c contains NaN? no.

Performance: active_search uses Python loop over idx (32) with slice ops; good.

Let's draft final code in analysis to check line count.

```
import numpy as np
import time

QUICK_TS = (0.0, 0.5, 1.0)

def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    s = float(h.sum())
    target = 0.5 * n
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
        return h
    if diff > 0.0:
        room = n - s
        if room <= 0.0:
            return h
        a = diff / room
        h = h + a * (1.0 - h)
    else:
        if s <= 0.0:
            return h
        a = diff / s
        h = h * (1.0 + a)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
```
One-step exact algebra. For negative diff, `h*(target/s)` because 1+diff/s = target/s. Good.

`compute_c5`:
```
def compute_c5(h,n):
   return float(np.max(np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full'))*(2.0/n))
```

```
def make_struct(n):
    M=2*n-1; d=n-1
    fidx=[]; fpar=[]; sidx=[]; spl=[]
    for p in range(n):
       lf=p if p>0 else 0? max(0,p) always p>=0. rf=min(M-1,p+n-1)
       if lf <= rf:
          ks=np.arange(lf,rf+1,dtype=np.int32)
          js=p+d-ks
          nd=(js>=0)&(js<n)&(ks!=d)
          fidx.append(ks[nd]); fpar.append(js[nd])
       else: empties
       ls=max(0,d-p); rs=min(M-1,M-1-p)
       ...
    return fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M
```
For p=0, lf=0. Good.

`eval_t`:
```
def eval_t(c,A,d,h0,t,temp):
    np.multiply(A, t-h0, out=temp)
    temp += c
    temp[d] -= t*t - h0*h0
    return float(np.max(temp))
```
Be careful: `temp = c + delta*A`; temp after `np.multiply` then += c. `c` not modified. It modifies temp; okay. For t=h0, temp=c +0, subtract 0; current max.

`cd_fast` with drift limit. Need pass `use_bias`? no.
```
def cd_fast(h, c, n, struct, deadline, grid_size=20, golden_iters=18):
    fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M = struct
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=False)? To avoid modifying original? We can modify in place; but best unchanged if not update. Better copy initially: h=h.copy(); c=c.copy()
    temp=np.empty(M); A=np.zeros(M)
    grid=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid_size)
    target=0.5*n
    tol=1e-11
    rng? not using random permutation? Use deterministic cyclic; but active search can handle. Maybe use simple perm = np.arange(n) and maybe reverse every other pass to vary? Use `perm = np.arange(n)`; if pass%2 reverse? creates copy. Could use `perm = np.arange(n -1 - ..., -1, -1)`? Simpler cyclic.
    while time.time() < deadline:
       current_max = float(np.max(c))
       if current_max <= 0: break
       sum_h = float(h.sum())
       drift_limit = max(3.0, 0.03*n)
       improved = 0
       # maybe loop 1000*n to allow time? Use while over p with modulo? Better for range.
       for p in range(n):
          if time.time() > deadline: break
          h0=float(h[p]); A.fill(0.0)
          fi=fidx[p]
          if fi.size: A[fi] += 1.0 - h[fpar[p]]
          si=sidx[p]
          if si.size: A[si] += -h[spl[p]]
          A[d]=1.0
          best_q=current_max; tq=h0
          for tqi in QUICK_TS:
             f=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,tqi,temp)
             if f < best_q: best_q=f; tq=tqi
          if best_q >= current_max - tol: continue
          best_t,best_f=line_search(c,A,d,h0,current_max,grid,temp,golden_iters)
          if best_f >= current_max - tol or best_t == h0: continue
          new_sum = sum_h + (best_t - h0)
          old_drift = abs(sum_h-target); new_drift = abs(new_sum-target)
          if new_drift > max(old_drift, drift_limit) + 1e-9 and (new_sum > target) == (new_sum - sum_h > 0):
             # maybe allow if it greatly improves? We can still accept if best_f < current_max - 0.5? Simpler reject.
             continue
          c += (best_t - h0)*A
          c[d] -= best_t*best_t - h0*h0
          h[p] = best_t
          sum_h = new_sum
          current_max = best_f
          improved += 1
       if improved:
          h = project_feasible(h,n)
          c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full').astype(float)
       else:
          break
    return h,c
```

`interpolate_h`:
```
def interpolate_h(h_old,n_old,n_new):
   if n_new == len(h_old): return h_old.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_old)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_new)
   h_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,h_old)
   return project_feasible(h_new,n_new)
```
For n_new smaller, interpolation okay.

Maybe use `np.asarray` in run to avoid copy? initial_h_values. Use `np.array(initial_h_values,dtype=float).copy()` okay.

`make_struct` loop:
```
def make_struct(n):
    M=2*n-1; d=n-1
    fidx=[]; fpar=[]; sidx=[]; spl=[]
    empty=np.empty(0,dtype=np.int32)
    for p in range(n):
        rf=min(M-1,p+n-1); lf=p
        if lf <= rf:
            ks=np.arange(lf,rf+1,dtype=np.int32)
            js=(p+d-ks).astype(np.int32)
            nd=(js>=0)&(js<n)&(ks!=d)
            fidx.append(ks[nd]); fpar.append(js[nd])
        else: fidx.append(empty); fpar.append(empty)
        ls=max(0,d-p); rs=min(M-1,M-1-p)
        if ls<=rs:
            ks=np.arange(ls,rs+1,dtype=np.int32)
            js=(ks-d+p).astype(np.int32)
            nd=(js>=0)&(js<n)&(ks!=d)
            sidx.append(ks[nd]); spl.append(js[nd])
        else: ...
    return fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M
```
`empty` reused; if appended same empty okay read-only. Good.

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    final_deadline = t0 + total - 5.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        h_best = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n_best = int(len(h_best))
    except Exception:
        h_best = np.full(400, 0.5)
        n_best = 400
    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, n_best)
    c_best = np.correlate(h_best, 1.0 - h_best, mode='full').astype(float)
    best_c = float(np.max(c_best)) * (2.0 / n_best)
    if final_deadline - time.time() < 5.0:
        return h_best, best_c, n_best
    exp_len = max(120.0, total - 180.0)
    explore_deadline = min(t0 + exp_len, final_deadline - 80.0)
    explore_deadline = max(explore_deadline, min(t0 + 30.0, final_deadline))
    dl1 = min(t0 + min(60.0, 0.15 * exp_len), explore_deadline)
    if dl1 > time.time():
       ...
```

```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    h=np.clip(np.asarray(h,dtype=float),0.0,1.0)
    s=float(h.sum()); t=0.5*n; d=t-s
    if d>0.0:
        room=n-s
        if room>0: h=h+(d/room)*(1.0-h)
    elif d<0.0:
        if s>0: h=h*(t/s)
    return np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)

def make_struct(n):
 M=2*n-1; d=n-1; fidx=[]; fpar=[]; sidx=[]; spl=[]; empty=np.empty(0,dtype=np.int32)
 for p in range(n):
  rf=min(M-1,p+n-1)
  ks=np.arange(p,rf+1,dtype=np.int32); js=(p+d-ks).astype(np.int32); nd=(js>=0)&(js<n)&(ks!=d)
  fidx.append(ks[nd]); fpar.append(js[nd])
  ls=max(0,d-p); rs=min(M-1,M-1-p)
  ks=np.arange(ls,rs+1,dtype=np.int32); js=(ks-d+p).astype(np.int32); nd=(js>=0)&(js<n)&(ks!=d)
  sidx.append(ks[nd]); spl.append(js[nd])
 return fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M
```

```
def cd_fast(h,c,n,struct,deadline,grid_size=20,golden_iters=18):
 fidx,fpar,sidx,spl,d,M=struct
 h=np.array(h,dtype=float); c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float)
 temp=np.empty(M); A=np.zeros(M); grid=np.linspace(0,1,grid_size); target=0.5*n; tol=1e-11; passes=0
 while time.time()<deadline:
  cur=float(np.max(c)); sh=float(h.sum()); lim=max(3.0,0.03*n); imp=0
  rng_range = range(n-1,-1,-1) if passes%2 else range(n)
  for p in rng_range:
   if time.time()>deadline: break
   h0=float(h[p]); A.fill(0.0); fi=fidx[p]; A[fi]+=1.0-h[fpar[p]]; si=sidx[p]; A[si]-=h[spl[p]]; A[d]=1.0
   bq=cur
   for tq in QUICK_TS:
    f=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,tq,temp)
    if f<bq: bq=f
   if bq>=cur-tol: continue
   bt,bf=line_search(c,A,d,h0,cur,grid,temp,golden_iters)
   if bf>=cur-tol or abs(bt-h0)<1e-14: continue
   ns=sh+bt-h0
   if abs(ns-target)>max(abs(sh-target),lim)+1e-9 and (ns>target)==(ns>sh): continue
   c+=(bt-h0)*A; c[d]-=bt*bt-h0*h0; h[p]=bt; sh=ns; cur=bf; imp+=1
  if imp:
   h=project_feasible(h,n); c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float)
  else: break
  passes+=1
 return h,c

```
def line_search(c,A,d,h0,cur,grid,temp,gi):
 best=cur; bt=float(h0); bi=0; m=grid.size
 for i in range(m):
  tg=float(grid[i]); f=eval_t(c,A,d,h0,tg,temp)
  if f<best: best=f; bt=tg; bi=i
 if 0<bi<m-1 and best<cur:
  a=float(grid[bi-1]); b=float(grid[bi+1]); gr=(np.sqrt(5.0)-1.0)/2.0
  c1=b-gr*(b-a); d1=a+gr*(b-a); fc=eval_t(...c1); fd=eval_t(...d1)
  for _ in range(gi):
   if fc<fd:
    b=d1; d1=c1; fd=fc; c1=b-gr*(b-a); fc=eval_t(...)
   else:
    a=c1; c1=d1; fc=fd; d1=a+gr*(b-a); fd=eval_t(...)
  tm=0.5*(a+b); fm=eval_t(...tm)
  if fm<best: best=fm; bt=tm
  if fc<best: best=fc; bt=c1
  if fd<best: best=fd; bt=d1
 return bt,best

active_search compress:
```
def active_search(h,c,n,deadline,k=24):
 h=np.array(h,dtype=float); c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h).astype(float); cur=float(np.max(c)); lr=2.0; no=0
 while time.time()<deadline:
  kk=min(k,c.size)
  if kk<c.size: idx=np.argpartition(c,kk-1)[-kk:]
  else: idx=np.arange(c.size)
  w=np.exp(6.0*(c[idx]-cur)); sw=w.sum()
  if sw<=0: w=np.ones(kk); sw=float(kk)
  else: w=w/sw
  g=np.zeros(n); d=n-1
  for j in range(len(idx)):
   wi=float(w[j]); m=int(idx[j]); diff=d-m
   if wi>1e-12:
    if diff>=0:
     L=n-diff; g[0:L]+=wi*(1.0-h[diff:n]); g[diff:n]-=wi*h[0:L]
    else:
     L=n+diff; g[-diff:n]+=wi*(1.0-h[0:L]); g[0:L]-=wi*h[-diff:n]
  g*=2.0/n
  step=lr; acc=False
  for _ in range(6):
   h2=project_feasible(h-step*g,n)
   c2=np.correlate(h2,1.0-h2).astype(float); mx=float(np.max(c2))
   if mx<cur-1e-12:
    h=h2; c=c2; cur=mx; lr=min(20.0, step*1.3); acc=True; break
   step*=0.5
  if acc: no=0
  else:
   no+=1
   if no>7: break
 return h,c,cur
```

 for nn in cands:

  if alloc<15: break
  htry=interpolate_h(h_best,n_best,nn)
  ctry=np.correlate(htry,1.0-htry).astype(float)

   del st

 # final
 if final-time.time()>40:

  if dl>time.time(): active final update
 if final-time.time()>10:

 h_best=project_feasible(h_best,n_best)

 return h_best,best_c,n_best

```python
import numpy as np
import time

QUICK_TS = (0.0, 0.5, 1.0)


def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    s = float(h.sum())
    t = 0.5 * n
    d = t - s
    if d > 0.0:
        room = n - s
        if room > 0.0:
            h = h + (d / room) * (1.0 - h)
    elif d < 0.0:
        if s > 0.0:
            h = h * (t / s)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def make_struct(n):
    m = 2 * n - 1
    d = n - 1
    fidx, fpar, sidx, spl = [], [], [], []
    for p in range(n):
        ks = np.arange(p, min(m - 1, p + n - 1) + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        js = (p + d - ks).astype(np.int32)
        nd = (js >= 0) & (js < n) & (ks != d)
        fidx.append(ks[nd])
        fpar.append(js[nd])
        ls = max(0, d - p)
        ks = np.arange(ls, min(m - 1, m - 1 - p) + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        js = (ks - d + p).astype(np.int32)
        nd = (js >= 0) & (js < n) & (ks != d)
        sidx.append(ks[nd])
        spl.append(js[nd])
    return fidx, fpar, sidx, spl, d, m


def eval_t(c, a, d, h0, t, temp):
    np.multiply(a, t - h0, out=temp)
    temp += c
    temp[d] -= t * t - h0 * h0
    return float(np.max(temp))


def line_search(c, a, d, h0, cur, grid, temp, gi):
    best = cur
    bt = float(h0)
    bi = 0
    mg = grid.size
    for i in range(mg):
        tg = float(grid[i])
        f = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, tg, temp)
        if f < best:
            best = f
            bt = tg
            bi = i
    if 0 < bi < mg - 1 and best < cur:
        lo = float(grid[bi - 1])
        hi = float(grid[bi + 1])
        gr = (np.sqrt(5.0) - 1.0) / 2.0
        a1 = hi - gr * (hi - lo)
        b1 = lo + gr * (hi - lo)
        fa = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, a1, temp)
        fb = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, b1, temp)
        for _ in range(gi):
            if fa < fb:
                hi = b1
                b1 = a1
                fb = fa
                a1 = hi - gr * (hi - lo)
                fa = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, a1, temp)
            else:
                lo = a1
                a1 = b1
                fa = fb
                b1 = lo + gr * (hi - lo)
                fb = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, b1, temp)
        tm = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        fm = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, tm, temp)
        if fm < best:
            best = fm
            bt = tm
        if fa < best:
            best = fa
            bt = a1
        if fb < best:
            best = fb
            bt = b1
    return bt, best


def cd_fast(h, c, n, struct, deadline, grid_size=20, golden_iters=18):
    fidx, fpar, sidx, spl, d, m = struct
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
    temp = np.empty(m)
    a = np.zeros(m)
    grid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid_size)
    target = 0.5 * n
    tol = 1e-11
    passes = 0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        cur = float(np.max(c))
        sh = float(h.sum())
        lim = max(3.0, 0.03 * n)
        imp = 0
        order = range(n - 1, -1, -1) if passes % 2 else range(n)
        for p in order:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            h0 = float(h[p])
            a.fill(0.0)
            a[fidx[p]] += 1.0 - h[fpar[p]]
            a[sidx[p]] -= h[spl[p]]
            a[d] = 1.0
            bq = cur
            for tq in QUICK_TS:
                f = eval_t(c, a, d, h0, tq, temp)
                if f < bq:
                    bq = f
            if bq >= cur - tol:
                continue
            bt, bf = line_search(c, a, d, h0, cur, grid, temp, golden_iters)
            if bf >= cur - tol or abs(bt - h0) < 1e-14:
                continue
            ns = sh + bt - h0
            if abs(ns - target) > max(abs(sh - target), lim) + 1e-9 and (ns > target) == (ns > sh):
                continue
            c += (bt - h0) * a
            c[d] -= bt * bt - h0 * h0
            h[p] = bt
            sh = ns
            cur = bf
            imp += 1
        if imp:
            h = project_feasible(h, n)
            c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
        else:
            break
        passes += 1
    return h, c


def active_search(h, c, n, deadline, k=24):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)
    cur = float(np.max(c))
    lr = 2.0
    no = 0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        kk = min(k, c.size)
        if kk < c.size:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, kk - 1)[-kk:]
        else:
            idx = np.arange(c.size)
        w = np.exp(6.0 * (c[idx] - cur))
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones(kk)
            sw = float(kk)
        else:
            w = w / sw
        g = np.zeros(n)
        d = n - 1
        for j in range(len(idx)):
            wi = float(w[j])
            if wi <= 1e-12:
                continue
            mlag = int(idx[j])
            diff = d - mlag
            if diff >= 0:
                length = n - diff
                g[0:length] += wi * (1.0 - h[diff:n])
                g[diff:n] -= wi * h[0:length]
            else:
                length = n + diff
                g[-diff:n] += wi * (1.0 - h[0:length])
                g[0:length] -= wi * h[-diff:n]
        g *= 2.0 / n
        step = lr
        acc = False
        for _ in range(6):
            h2 = project_feasible(h - step * g, n)
            c2 = np.correlate(h2, 1.0 - h2, mode="full").astype(float)
            mx = float(np.max(c2))
            if mx < cur - 1e-12:
                h = h2
                c = c2
                cur = mx
                lr = min(20.0, step * 1.3)
                acc = True
                break
            step *= 0.5
        if acc:
            no = 0
        else:
            no += 1
            if no > 7:
                break
    return h, c, cur


def interpolate_h(h_old, n_old, n_new):
    if n_new == len(h_old):
        return np.array(h_old, dtype=float).copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new)
    h_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old)
    return project_feasible(h_new, n_new)


def better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try):
    n_try = len(h_try)
    if n_try <= 0:
        return h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c
    h_feas = project_feasible(h_try, n_try)
    c_feas = np.correlate(h_feas, 1.0 - h_feas, mode="full").astype(float)
    val = float(np.max(c_feas)) * (2.0 / n_try)
    if val < best_c - 1e-15:
        return h_feas, c_feas, n_try, val
    return h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    final = t0 + total - 5.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        h_best = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n_best = int(len(h_best))
    except Exception:
        h_best = np.full(400, 0.5)
        n_best = 400
    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, n_best)
    c_best = np.correlate(h_best, 1.0 - h_best, mode="full").astype(float)
    best_c = float(np.max(c_best)) * (2.0 / n_best)
    if final - time.time() < 10.0:
        return h_best, best_c, n_best

    expl = max(120.0, total - 180.0)
    explore = min(t0 + expl, final - 80.0)
    explore = max(explore, min(t0 + 30.0, final))

    dl = min(t0 + min(60.0, 0.15 * expl), explore)
    if dl > time.time():
        h_try, c_try, _ = active_search(h_best, c_best, n_best, dl, k=24)
        h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)

    dl = min(t0 + min(300.0, 0.55 * expl), explore)
    if dl > time.time():
        st = make_struct(n_best)
        h_try, c_try = cd_fast(
            h_best, c_best, n_best, st, dl,
            24 if n_best <= 600 else 18,
            22 if n_best <= 600 else 16
        )
        h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)
        del st

    dl = min(t0 + min(420.0, 0.70 * expl), explore)
    if dl > time.time():
        h_try, c_try, _ = active_search(h_best, c_best, n_best, dl, k=24)
        h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)

    if time.time() < explore - 70.0:
        for sig in (0.03, 0.08):
            rem = explore - time.time()
            if rem < 60.0:
                break
            alloc = min(50.0, 0.25 * rem)
            h_np = project_feasible(h_best + sig * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n_best), n_best)
            c_np = np.correlate(h_np, 1.0 - h_np, mode="full").astype(float)
            st = make_struct(n_best)
            h_try, c_try = cd_fast(h_np, c_np, n_best, st, time.time() + alloc, 20, 18)
            h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)
            del st

    cands = (700, 800, 1000, 900, 600, 400, 300, 250)
    for nn in cands:
        if nn == n_best or nn < 100 or nn > 1000:
            continue
        rem = explore - time.time()
        if rem < 70.0:
            break
        default = 130.0 if nn > 850 else (100.0 if nn > 650 else 80.0)
        alloc = min(default, max(30.0, 0.18 * rem))
        if rem - alloc < 40.0:
            alloc = max(20.0, rem - 60.0)
        if alloc < 15.0:
            break
        h_try = interpolate_h(h_best, n_best, nn)
        c_try = np.correlate(h_try, 1.0 - h_try, mode="full").astype(float)
        at = min(30.0, 0.35 * alloc)
        if at > 5.0:
            h_try, c_try, _ = active_search(h_try, c_try, nn, time.time() + at, k=20)
        if time.time() < explore - 10.0:
            st = make_struct(nn)
            h_try, c_try = cd_fast(
                h_try, c_try, nn, st, time.time() + max(10.0, alloc - at),
                18 if nn <= 600 else 14,
                16 if nn <= 600 else 12
            )
            del st
        h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)

    if final - time.time() > 40.0:
        dl = min(time.time() + min(25.0, 0.25 * (final - time.time())), final - 20.0)
        if dl > time.time():
            h_try, c_try, _ = active_search(h_best, c_best, n_best, dl, k=24)
            h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)

    if final - time.time() > 10.0:
        st = make_struct(n_best)
        h_try, c_try = cd_fast(
            h_best, c_best, n_best, st, final,
            30 if n_best <= 600 else 20,
            28 if n_best <= 600 else 20
        )
        h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c = better_solution(h_best, c_best, n_best, best_c, h_try, c_try)
        del st

    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, n_best)
    c_best = np.correlate(h_best, 1.0 - h_best, mode="full").astype(float)
    best_c = float(np.max(c_best)) * (2.0 / n_best)
    return h_best, best_c, int(n_best)
```
